---
name = "hex0"
defaultTargets = ["Spec", "RawAsm", "LowIR", "LowSSA"]

# The language tower is one library per top-level directory. Each lib is rooted
# at an aggregator module (e.g. `RawAsm.lean` imports the rung's main entry
# points) and claims its whole namespace subtree with a submodule glob
# ("Ns.*"), so every module is claimed by exactly one lib (the disjoint
# top-level namespaces guarantee no overlap). Cross-lib import dependencies
# (RawAsm → Spec, LowIR → Spec/RawAsm, LowSSA → LowIR) are resolved by Lake's
# global module graph. Build a whole rung with `lake build <Lib>`, or a single
# module with `lake build <Module.Name>`.
#
#   Spec/     — functional specifications, the refinement targets (rung-independent)
#   RawAsm/   — bare RV64I model + hex0/hex1 verified at the flat-PC altitude
#   LowIR/    — structured IL (Core → Ctrl → Prog) + verified compiler + ProgSim
#   LowSSA/   — the SSA variant of the Prog IR (docs/LOWIR-SSA-EXPERIMENT.md)
#
# (`lean/DumpProgMain.lean` lives outside every namespace on purpose — it is a
# `lake env lean --run` tool, not a library module, so no lib claims it.)

[[lean_lib]]
name = "Spec"
globs = ["Spec.*"]

[[lean_lib]]
name = "RawAsm"
globs = ["RawAsm.*"]

[[lean_lib]]
name = "LowIR"
globs = ["LowIR.*"]

[[lean_lib]]
name = "LowSSA"
globs = ["LowSSA.*"]

# --- back-compat target aliases -------------------------------------------
# The four libs above ARE the tower (and the default build). The aliases below
# preserve the pre-reorg `lake build <name>` invocations documented in the
# handoff/experiment docs. They are NOT in defaultTargets; each roots a subset
# of the tower, and its cross-subtree dependencies resolve through the glob
# libs above (Lake builds a single global module graph). Overlapping claims are
# fine — a module builds once and both libs share the olean.

[[lean_lib]]
name = "Hex0"                       # the hex0 rung program (was lean/Hex0/*)
roots = ["RawAsm.Hex0"]

[[lean_lib]]
name = "Hex1"                       # the hex1 rung program (was lean/Hex1/*)
roots = ["RawAsm.Hex1"]

[[lean_lib]]
name = "LowIRCompile"               # the D7/D8 compiler cut + differential tests
roots = ["LowIR.CompileTests"]

[[lean_lib]]
name = "LowIRProgSim"               # the compile_sim-for-Prog proof campaign
roots = ["LowIR.ProgSim.Defs", "LowIR.ProgSim.SlotFacts", "LowIR.ProgSim.MemFacts", "LowIR.ProgSim.StmtSim", "LowIR.ProgSim.CtrlSim"]
```

1	/-
2	  LowIR.Prog — the D7/D8 IR: LowIR.Ctrl's structured control flow plus
3	  activation-local CALLS and per-function stack FRAMES (LOWIR-DESIGN.md D7/D8).
4	
5	  D7 (calls): a program is an `Env : Name → FunDef`. A call runs the callee's
6	  body in a FRESH zero-initialized register file with only the parameters bound
7	  to the caller's argument values; on return, only the declared return registers
8	  are copied back into the caller's file. Call sites carry their own arities
9	  (arity-indexed `Vector`s, fixed-size by construction); `wf` cross-checks them
10	  against the env. NOT SSA — registers are mutable locals.
11	
12	  D8 (frames): `St` carries a semantic, program-unwritable stack pointer `sp`.
13	  Entering a function subtracts its `frameSize` (overflow below `stackLo` →
14	  `none`) and binds the new frame base to the function's designated `frameReg`
15	  (an ordinary IL register — the ONLY way a program observes sp). Returning
16	  restores the caller's `sp` structurally. No `alloca`.
17	
18	  Also new over Ctrl: `ld`/`sd` (64-bit little-endian load/store, byte order
19	  identical to `Rv64i.State.loadWord/storeWord`) and `annot` (Ext. 9 — a
20	  semantic no-op carrying a string).
21	
22	  `LowIR.Ctrl` is untouched (hex0 proofs stay green); `Outcome` is reused.
23	-/
24	import LowIR.Ctrl
25	
26	namespace LowIR.Prog
27	
28	open LowIR (Cond evalCond)
29	open LowIR.Ctrl (Outcome)
30	open Rv64i (Word Byte)
31	
32	abbrev Reg := Nat
33	abbrev Name := String
34	
35	/-- Structured statements. Ctrl's constructs minus its toy `call (g : Stmt)`,
36	    plus `ld`/`sd`/`annot` and the D7 named call. -/
37	inductive Stmt where
38	  | skip
39	  | seq    (a b : Stmt)
40	  | addi   (rd rs : Reg) (imm : BitVec 12)
41	  | add    (rd rs1 rs2 : Reg)
42	  | sub    (rd rs1 rs2 : Reg)
43	  | orr    (rd rs1 rs2 : Reg)
44	  | slli   (rd rs : Reg) (sh : Nat)
45	  | srli   (rd rs : Reg) (sh : Nat)
46	  | lbu    (rd rs : Reg) (imm : BitVec 12)
47	  | sb     (rbase rval : Reg) (imm : BitVec 12)
48	  | ld     (rd rs : Reg) (imm : BitVec 12)          -- rd := mem64[rs + sext imm]
49	  | sd     (rbase rval : Reg) (imm : BitVec 12)     -- mem64[rbase + sext imm] := rval
50	  | annot  (a : String)                             -- semantic no-op (Ext. 9)
51	  | ife    (c : Cond) (a b : Reg) (t e : Stmt)
52	  | while  (c : Cond) (a b : Reg) (body : Stmt)     -- continue scope
53	  | block  (body : Stmt)                            -- break scope
54	  | brkB   (k : Nat)
55	  | contL  (k : Nat)
56	  | ret
57	  | call   (argc rvc : Nat) (f : Name)
58	           (args : Vector Reg argc) (rets : Vector Reg rvc)
59	  | cref   (rd : Reg) (d : Name)     -- rd := address of const data object d
60	  | clen   (rd : Reg) (d : Name)     -- rd := length of const data object d
61	deriving Repr
62	
63	/-- A function: declared parameter/return registers (callee-side names), a
64	    frame size in bytes, the register that receives the frame base, a body. -/
65	structure FunDef where
66	  argc      : Nat
67	  rvc       : Nat
68	  params    : Vector Reg argc
69	  rets      : Vector Reg rvc
70	  frameSize : Nat
71	  frameReg  : Reg
72	  body      : Stmt
73	
74	abbrev Env := List (Name × FunDef)
75	
76	/-- The program's read-only data segment: named constant byte objects,
77	    referenced from code as slices via `cref` (address) + `clen` (length). -/
78	abbrev Data := List (Name × List Byte)
79	
80	/-- A whole program: functions plus const data. Where the data objects LIVE
81	    is not part of the program — the semantics takes a base map (`dbase`),
82	    ∀-quantifiable like D8's `sp₀`, so programs stay address-independent. -/
83	structure Program where
84	  env  : Env
85	  data : Data
86	
87	/-- Data-free view (keeps pre-data call sites unchanged). -/
88	instance : Coe Env Program := ⟨fun env => ⟨env, []⟩⟩
89	
90	/-- IL machine state: register file (x0 hardwired 0), byte memory, and the
91	    semantic stack pointer — no instruction writes `sp`; only call/return move i

1	/-
2	  LowIR.Compile — Prog → RV64I instructions (EXECUTABLE, UNVERIFIED first cut).
3	
4	  Strategy: MEMORY-LOCALS (-O0). Every IL register gets an 8-byte slot in the
5	  function's machine frame; no register allocation. Correctness-first — the
6	  point is a differential-testing target for the D7/D8 semantics, and later the
7	  subject of `compile_sim`-style theorems (performance is a non-goal, N9).
8	
9	  Machine frame (grows down; all offsets sp-relative, sp = x2 after prologue):
10	
11	        sp + 0                  : saved ra
12	        sp + 8*(1+r)            : slot for IL register r   (r = 0..maxReg)
13	        sp + 8*(maxReg+2)       : user frame (frameSize bytes) — the address
14	                                  handed to the IL `frameReg`
15	        total = 8*(maxReg+2) + frameSize   (must fit imm12: ≤ 2000, checked)
16	
17	  Physical registers: x2=sp, x1=ra, x5/x6 = t0/t1 scratch, x10..x17 = a0..a7
18	  argument/return marshalling (so argc,rvc ≤ 8, checked). Nothing is live
19	  across an IL statement except the slots, so calls need no caller-save logic.
20	
21	  IL zero-init is matched EXACTLY: the prologue `sd x0` into every non-param,
22	  non-frameReg slot (differential tests stay honest; definite-assignment
23	  optimization comes later).
24	
25	  Pipeline: lower to `SymInstr` (labels symbolic, calls by name) → lay out the
26	  whole program (all instructions are 4 bytes) → resolve branches/jumps/calls
27	  to relative offsets (range-checked) → `Rv64i.Instr` list. Encoding to bytes
28	  reuses `LowIR.encode`/`asmBytes`. Entry convention: the program starts with
29	  a single `jal ra, <entry>` stub at `codeBase`; when the entry function
30	  returns, pc = codeBase+4 — run the machine with `runFuel (codeBase+4)`.
31	-/
32	import LowIR.Prog
33	
34	namespace LowIR.Compile
35	
36	open LowIR (Cond condInstr jal0)
37	open LowIR.Prog (Reg Name FunDef Env Data Program wfProgram pad8 dataOffsetsFrom dataSegment)
38	open Rv64i (Word Byte Instr)
39	
40	/-- The IL being compiled (the parent `LowIR.Stmt` also exists — be explicit). -/
41	local notation "PStmt" => LowIR.Prog.Stmt
42	
43	/-! ### Physical register roles -/
44	
45	def SP : Reg := 2
46	def RA : Reg := 1
47	def T0 : Reg := 5
48	def T1 : Reg := 6
49	/-- Argument/return register `a_i` = x10+i. -/
50	def A (i : Nat) : Reg := 10 + i
51	
52	/-! ### Symbolic instructions -/
53	
54	/-- Position-independent instruction stream: concrete instructions, label
55	    markers (emit nothing), and label/name-relative control transfers. -/
56	inductive SymInstr where
57	  | ins   (i : Instr)                          -- concrete, position-independent
58	  | label (l : Nat)                            -- marker (0 bytes)
59	  | br    (c : Cond) (a b : Reg) (l : Nat)     -- branch (phys regs) to label
60	  | jmp   (l : Nat)                            -- jal x0, label
61	  | callf (f : Name)                           -- jal ra, function
62	  | cref  (d : Name)                           -- T0 := address of data object
63	deriving Repr
64	
65	/-! ### Per-function lowering -/
66	
67	/-- Slot offset of IL register `r` (see frame picture above). -/
68	def slotOff (r : Reg) : Nat := 8 * (1 + r)
69	
70	/-- Load IL register `r`'s slot into physical register `t`
71	    (IL x0 is hardwired zero — never read its slot). -/
72	def loadSlot (r : Reg) (t : Reg) : List SymInstr :=
73	  if r = 0 then [.ins (.addi t 0 0)]
74	  else [.ins (.ld t SP (BitVec.ofNat 12 (slotOff r)))]

76	/-- Store physical register `t` into IL register `r`'s slot

78	def storeSlot (r : Reg) (t : Reg) : List SymInstr :=
79	  if r = 0 then []
80	  else [.ins (.sd SP t (BitVec.ofNat 12 (slotOff r)))]

1	/-
2	  LowIR.ProgSim.CtrlSim — the label-aware lowering `emitCF` and its size `csize`,
3	  the position/label-resolved extension of `emit` to control flow.
4	
5	  `emit` (StmtSim) is label-free and returns `[]` for the control-flow ops. This
6	  file resolves them: given the enclosing break/continue label POSITIONS
7	  (`brkPos`/`contPos`), the epilogue position (`epiPos`), and the byte offset
8	  `here` where this statement's code begins, `emitCF` produces the concrete RV64I
9	  stream the real `Compile.lower ▸ layout ▸ resolveOne` pipeline emits — every
10	  `.jmp`/`.br` a single `jal x0`/branch whose offset is `target − here`, every
11	  label 0 bytes. The internal `ife`/`while` label offsets are position-INDEPENDENT
12	  (they depend only on sub-block sizes, `csize`); only `ret`/`brkB`/`contL` targets
13	  (external labels) depend on `here`.
14	
15	  This is the label-aware `emit` the outcome-carrying `lower_sim` (next) inducts
16	  over — and the seed of the validated IR↔assembly mapping (Dump.lean renders the
17	  tree; `emitCF`/`csize` annotate it with byte positions).
18	-/
19	import LowIR.ProgSim.StmtSim
20	import LowIR.Lib
21	
22	namespace LowIR.ProgSim
23	
24	open Rv64i (Instr State step Word decode fetch32)
25	open LowIR.Compile (T0 T1 userOff slotOff maxRegF maxRegS)
26	open LowIR.Ctrl (Outcome)
27	open LowIR.Prog (St Program Name FunDef Data dbaseOf)
28	open LowIR (Cond evalCond condInstr jal0)

30	local notation "PStmt" => LowIR.Prog.Stmt
31	
32	/-- Instruction count of a statement's lowering — position/label-independent
33	    (every jump/branch is one 4-byte instruction, every label 0 bytes). The
34	    straight-line cases delegate to `emit` (already `#guard`'d faithful to
35	    `Compile.lower`); the control-flow overheads are read off the compiler's
36	    layout: `ife` = 2 slot-loads + branch + jmp (4), `while` = +1 back-edge (5).
37	    `cref`/`clen` are `0` here (placeholder — `emit` still stubs them). -/
38	def csize : PStmt → Nat
39	  | .seq a b        => csize a + csize b
40	  | .ret            => 1
41	  | .brkB _         => 1
42	  | .contL _        => 1
43	  | .block body     => csize body
44	  | .ife _ _ _ t e  => 4 + csize e + csize t
45	  | .while _ _ _ b  => 5 + csize b
46	  | .clen rd _      => 3 + (if rd = 0 then 0 else 1)   -- synthConst (3) + storeSlot
47	  | .cref rd _      => 5 + (if rd = 0 then 0 else 1)   -- pc-read + synth + add + store
48	  | s               => (emit s).length
49	
50	/-- The label-aware lowering. `brkPos`/`contPos` are the enclosing block-end /
51	    loop-top label byte positions (indexed like `brkB`/`contL`'s de-Bruijn
52	    indices); `epiPos` the epilogue; `here` the byte offset of this code.
53	    Straight-line cases reuse `emit` (`here` irrelevant). -/
54	def emitCF (dat : Data) (dpos : Name → Nat) (brkPos contPos : List Nat) (epiPos : Nat) :
55	    Nat → PStmt → List Instr
56	  | here, .seq a b =>
57	      emitCF dat dpos brkPos contPos epiPos here a ++
58	        emitCF dat dpos brkPos contPos epiPos (here + 4 * csize a) b
59	  | here, .ret     => [LowIR.jal0 ((epiPos : Int) - (here : Int))]
60	  | here, .brkB k  => [LowIR.jal0 ((brkPos.getD k 0 : Int) - (here : Int))]
61	  | here, .contL k => [LowIR.jal0 ((contPos.getD k 0 : Int) - (here : Int))]
62	  | here, .block body =>
63	      -- the break target `lEnd` sits just past the body (0-byte label).
64	      emitCF dat dpos ((here + 4 * csize body) :: brkPos) contPos epiPos here body
65	  | here, .ife c a b t e =>
66	      -- br→lT (then) skips over the else block; jmp→lEnd skips over then.
67	      -- offsets are size-relative: lT is 8 + 4|e| past the branch, lEnd 4 + 4|t|
68	      -- past the jmp. else code `e` runs on

1	/-
2	  LowIR.ProgSim.Defs — the `compile_sim`-for-Prog relation and its executable
3	  scaffolding (RESUME-PROGSIM §3). This file is the FOUNDATION of the D7/D8
4	  compiler-correctness campaign: the simulation `Layout`/`Installed`/`StInv`,
5	  the write-footprint instrumentation `execT`, and (in ProgSim/Main, later) the
6	  top-level theorems. Everything here is a real, `sorry`-free DEFINITION plus a
7	  handful of `sorry`'d equivalence theorems whose statements are the deliverable
8	  — the proofs land in later phases.
9	
10	  Discipline (RESUME-PROGSIM §6): every relation piece gets an EXECUTABLE
11	  sanity check (`#guard`) against real compiled programs BEFORE any lemma leans
12	  on it. This file's #guards compile fixtures through the FROZEN compiler, load
13	  the bytes into the trusted `Rv64i` machine, and check the relation's
14	  computable shadow holds — a wrong def is caught here in seconds, not inside
15	  Phase 5 in weeks.
16	
17	  Commit A (this file, first cut): `Layout`, `layoutOf`, `Installed` + the
18	  computable `codeInstalledB`/`dataInstalledB`, `execT` + `execT_erase`. The
19	  relation `StInv`/`MachPriv`/`SimPre` and the `lower_sim`/`call_sim`/`prog_sim`
20	  statements arrive in a follow-up commit.
21	-/
22	import LowIR.Compile
23	import LowIR.ProgSim.ExecFacts
24	import LowIR.ProgSim.WordMem
25	
26	namespace LowIR.ProgSim
27	
28	open LowIR (Cond evalCond)

30	open LowIR.Prog (Reg Name FunDef Env Data Program frameEnter
31	                 dataSegment dataOffsetsFrom pad8 dbaseOf installData)
32	open LowIR.Compile (compileProgT progBytes userOff totalFrame slotOff maxRegF)
33	open Rv64i (Word Byte Instr State)
34	
35	-- `St`/`exec`/`Stmt` collide with the Ctrl-level `LowIR.St`/`LowIR.exec`
36	-- (enclosing-namespace lookup wins over `open LowIR.Prog`), so alias the Prog
37	-- ones locally and qualify the rest.
38	local notation "PStmt" => LowIR.Prog.Stmt
39	abbrev St := LowIR.Prog.St
40	
41	/-! ## §3.1 (part) — where the code and data live: `Layout` and `Installed` -/
42	
43	/-- The compiled artifact's placement, per RESUME-PROGSIM §3.1: the base
44	    address, the resolved instruction stream (`= (compileProgT P entry).1`), the
45	    function→byte-offset table, the byte offset where the data segment starts
46	    inside the blob (`pad8 (4 * #instrs)` — every instruction is 4 bytes after
47	    resolution), and the program's const data. -/
48	structure Layout where
49	  codeBase : Word
50	  instrs   : List Instr
51	  fnTab    : List (Name × Nat)
52	  segStart : Nat
53	  data     : Data
54	deriving Repr
55	
56	/-- Build the `Layout` for `entry` at `codeBase` straight from the FROZEN
57	    compiler. `none` iff the program is uncompilable (same condition as
58	    `compileProgT`). `segStart` is recomputed here from the resolved stream
59	    and is DEFINITIONALLY the compiler's `pad8 codeEnd` (proved in Phase 2). -/
60	def layoutOf (P : Program) (entry : Name) (codeBase : Word) : Option Layout :=
61	  match compileProgT P entry with
62	  | some (is, fns, _) =>
63	      some { codeBase, instrs := is, fnTab := fns,
64	             segStart := pad8 (4 * is.length), data := P.data }
65	  | none => none
66	
67	/-- The machine halt pc: the self-loop landing pad the compiler puts at
68	    `codeBase+4` (the entry stub is `jal ra,entry` at `codeBase`; the entry
69	    returns to `codeBase+4`). Run the machine with `runFuel L.haltPc`. -/
70	def Layout.haltPc (L : Layout) : Word := L.codeBase + 4
71	
72	/-- Address one past the last instruction — the code window's top. -/
73	def Layout.codeEnd (L : Layout) : Word :=
74	  L.codeBase + BitVec.ofNat 64 (4 * L.instrs.length)
75

45	/-! ## The resolved straight-line lowering `emit`. -/
46	
47	/-- Resolved `loadSlot`: physical reg `t := IL reg r` (r=0 ⇒ materialise 0). -/
48	def loadSlotI (r t : Nat) : List Instr :=
49	  if r = 0 then [.addi t 0 0] else [.ld t SP (BitVec.ofNat 12 (slotOff r))]
50	
51	/-- Resolved `storeSlot`: IL reg `r := physical reg t` (r=0 ⇒ discard). -/
52	def storeSlotI (r t : Nat) : List Instr :=
53	  if r = 0 then [] else [.sd SP t (BitVec.ofNat 12 (slotOff r))]
54	
55	/-- The resolved instruction stream for a straight-line statement — mirrors
56	    `Compile.lower` exactly for the label-free cases (validated by `#guard`
57	    against the real pipeline below). Control-flow/`cref`/`clen` are `[]` here;
58	    their `lower_sim` cases are `sorry` (they use the real `Emitted`, not `emit`). -/
59	def emit : PStmt → List Instr
60	  | .skip            => []
61	  | .annot _         => []
62	  | .addi rd rs imm  => loadSlotI rs T0 ++ [.addi T0 T0 imm] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
63	  | .add  rd r1 r2   => loadSlotI r1 T0 ++ loadSlotI r2 T1 ++ [.add T0 T0 T1] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
64	  | .sub  rd r1 r2   => loadSlotI r1 T0 ++ loadSlotI r2 T1 ++ [.sub T0 T0 T1] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
65	  | .orr  rd r1 r2   => loadSlotI r1 T0 ++ loadSlotI r2 T1 ++ [.or  T0 T0 T1] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
66	  | .slli rd rs sh   => loadSlotI rs T0 ++ [.slli T0 T0 sh] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
67	  | .srli rd rs sh   => loadSlotI rs T0 ++ [.srli T0 T0 sh] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
68	  | .lbu  rd rs imm  => loadSlotI rs T0 ++ [.lbu T0 T0 imm] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
69	  | .ld   rd rs imm  => loadSlotI rs T0 ++ [.ld  T0 T0 imm] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
70	  | .sb   rb rv imm  => loadSlotI rb T0 ++ loadSlotI rv T1 ++ [.sb T0 T1 imm]
71	  | .sd   rb rv imm  => loadSlotI rb T0 ++ loadSlotI rv T1 ++ [.sd T0 T1 imm]
72	  | .seq a b         => emit a ++ emit b
73	  | _                => []
74	
75	/-! ## `Emitted` — the static "code segment carries `is` at `pos`" fact. -/
76	
77	/-- `L.instrs` has `is` as its segment starting at byte offset `pos`
78	    (`pos` 4-aligned; each `is[j]` is `L.instrs[pos/4 + j]`). Static in `L`; the
79	    machine fetch is recovered from `Installed` (a `StInv` conjunct). -/
80	def Emitted (L : Layout) (pos : Nat) (is : List Instr) : Prop :=
81	  pos % 4 = 0 ∧ ∀ (j : Nat) (h : j < is.length),
82	    ∃ (h2 : pos / 4 + j < L.instrs.length), L.instrs[pos / 4 + j] = is[j]'h
83	
84	/-- `Emitted` of a concatenation splits: `is₁` at `pos`, `is₂` at `pos + 4|is₁|`. -/

1	/-
2	  LowIR.ProgSim.MemFacts — the `StInv` algebra for the IL memory ops
3	  (RESUME-PROGSIM Phase 4.2). Where SlotFacts handled the compiler's PRIVATE frame
4	  slots (`storeWord` at `sp + slotOff r`, mirroring `rset`), this file handles the
5	  USER memory ops (`ld`/`sd`/`lbu`/`sb`): the IL and the machine touch the SAME
6	  address (the P1 payoff — `x2 ≡ sp`, so the slot-loaded base + imm equals the IL
7	  address literally), and correctness needs only that the touched bytes lie off the
8	  machine-private region (the frame hole + code blob).
9	
10	  Two ingredients:
11	    * agreement — `St.loadWord`/`State.loadWord` are byte-identical, so equal base
12	      bytes give equal loads (`loadWord_agree`); a `storeWord`/`storeByte` writes
13	      identical bytes into IL and machine memory, so it preserves agreement pointwise
14	      (`storeWord_mem_agree` — the key: no in-range/out-range split, since the stored
15	      bytes are literally the same expression on both sides);
16	    * preservation — a user `storeWord`/`storeByte` whose range is off THIS
17	      activation's hole (⇒ disjoint from every live slot) and off the blob
18	      (⇒ `Installed` survives) preserves `StInv`, with `s.storeWord addr v` on the
19	      IL side (`StInv_storeWord_user`/`StInv_storeByte_user`).
20	
21	  Register binders are `Nat` (a `≤` at `Reg = abbrev Nat` hides from `omega`).
22	  `@[inline]` note: `St.storeByte`/`St.loadByte` don't unfold via `simp [name]`; use
23	  the FULL name with `unfold` (the opened alias fails too). Kept `[propext, Quot.sound]`.
24	-/
25	import LowIR.ProgSim.SlotFacts
26	
27	namespace LowIR.ProgSim
28	
29	open LowIR.Prog (Name FunDef St)
30	open LowIR.Compile (userOff slotOff maxRegF)
31	open Rv64i (Word Byte State)
32	
33	/-! ## §1 — load/store byte agreement (IL ↔ machine, same base bytes). -/
34	
35	/-- `St.loadWord` and `State.loadWord` are the same little-endian read, so if the
36	    eight base bytes `a … a+7` agree, the 64-bit loads agree. -/
37	theorem loadWord_agree (s : St) (m : State) (a : Word)
38	    (h0 : s.mem a = m.mem a) (h1 : s.mem (a + 1) = m.mem (a + 1))
39	    (h2 : s.mem (a + 2) = m.mem (a + 2)) (h3 : s.mem (a + 3) = m.mem (a + 3))
40	    (h4 : s.mem (a + 4) = m.mem (a + 4)) (h5 : s.mem (a + 5) = m.mem (a + 5))
41	    (h6 : s.mem (a + 6) = m.mem (a + 6)) (h7 : s.mem (a + 7) = m.mem (a + 7)) :
42	    s.loadWord a = m.loadWord a := by
43	  simp only [St.loadWord, State.loadWord, h0, h1, h2, h3, h4, h5, h6, h7]
44	
45	/-- Single-byte agreement (both are `.mem a` definitionally). -/
46	theorem loadByte_agree (s : St) (m : State) (a : Word) (h : s.mem a = m.mem a) :
47	    s.loadByte a = m.loadByte a := h
48	
49	/-- A `loadWord` depends only on the byte memory, so equal `.mem` gives equal loads. -/
50	theorem loadWord_mem_congr (m1 m2 : State) (a : Word) (h : m1.mem = m2.mem) :
51	    m1.loadWord a = m2.loadWord a := by simp only [State.loadWord, h]
52	
53	/-- The load-value payoff: at an address whose eight bytes are off `MachPriv`, the
54	    machine load equals the IL load (`StInv` gives byte agreement there). -/
55	theorem loadWord_agree_off (L : Layout) (fd : FunDef) (holes : List Hole) (s : St) (m : State)
56	    (addr : Word) (hinv : StInv L fd holes s m)
57	    (hoff : ∀ i, i < 8 → ¬ MachPriv L holes (addr + BitVec.ofNat 64 i)) :
58	    m.loadWord addr = s.loadWord addr := by
59	  have h4 := hinv.2.2.2.1
60	  have e0 : addr + BitVec.ofNat 64 0 = addr := by simp
61	  refine (loadWord_agree s m addr ?_ ?_ ?_ ?_ ?_ ?_ ?_ ?_).symm
62	  · exact h4 addr (e0 ▸ hoff 0 (by omega))
63	  · exact h4 (addr + 1) (hoff 1 (by omega))
64	  · exact h4 (addr + 2) (hoff 2 (by omega))
65	  · exact h4 (addr + 3) (hoff 3 (by omega))
66	  · exact h4 (addr + 4) (hoff 4 (by omega))
67	  · exact h4 (addr + 5) (hoff 5 (by omega))
68	  · exact h4 (addr + 6) (hoff 6 (by omega))
69	  · exact h4 (addr + 7) (hoff 7 (by omega))
70	
71	/-- A `storeWord` writes identical bytes into IL and machine memory, so wherever the
72

158	/-! ## Validation against the real compiler pipeline.
159	
160	    `emitCF` must equal what the trusted `Compile.lower ▸ layoutItems ▸ resolveOne`
161	    actually emits. `realResolve` runs that pipeline on a statement (no data, no
162	    outer scopes, from byte 0), and the `#guard`s check equality on the library's
163	    control-flow functions — the concrete, decidable "validated mapping". -/
164	open LowIR.Compile (lower layoutItems resolveOne)
165	
166	/-- The real resolved RV64I stream for a body, lowered in isolation from byte 0.
167	    Epilogue label `0` (targeted by `ret`) is planted just past the body — its

169	    can't collide with it. So `emitCF` must be compared with `epiPos = 4·csize`. -/
170	def realResolve (dat : Data) (dats : List (Name × Nat)) (body : PStmt) : Option (List Instr) :=
171	  let syms : List LowIR.Compile.SymInstr := (lower dat [] [] 0 body).run' 1
172	  let (flat, lbls, _) := layoutItems (syms ++ [LowIR.Compile.SymInstr.label 0]) 0
173	  (flat.mapM (fun p => resolveOne lbls [] dats p)).map List.flatten
174	
175	/-- `emitCF` matched against the real pipeline, epilogue at the body's end.
176	    `dpos d = (dats.lookup d).getD 0` mirrors `resolveOne`'s data-offset read. -/
177	def matchesReal (dat : Data) (dats : List (Name × Nat)) (body : PStmt) : Bool :=
178	  realResolve dat dats body
179	    == some (emitCF dat (fun d => (dats.lookup d).getD 0) [] [] (4 * csize body) 0 body)

71:  | jalr (rd rs1 : Nat) (imm : BitVec 12)
130:  | 0x67 => if funct3 = 0 then .jalr rd rs1 immI else .unknown
189:  | .jalr rd rs1 imm =>
---step_jal in StmtSim:
theorem step_jal (m : State) (rd : Nat) (imm : BitVec 21)
    (h : decode (fetch32 m) = .jal rd imm) :
    step m = (m.rset rd (m.pc + 4)).setPc (m.pc + imm.signExtend 64) := by simp only [step, h]

theorem step_beq (m : State) (a b : Nat) (imm : BitVec 13)
    (h : decode (fetch32 m) = .beq a b imm) :
    step m = m.setPc (if m.rget a = m.rget b then m.pc + imm.signExtend 64 else m.pc + 4) := by
  simp only [step, h]

theorem step_blt (m : State) (a b : Nat) (imm : BitVec 13)
    (h : decode (fetch32 m) = .blt a b imm) :
    step m = m.setPc (if (m.rget a).slt (m.rget b) then m.pc + imm.signExtend 64 else m.pc + 4) := by
  simp only [step, h]

theorem step_bge (m : State) (a b : Nat) (imm : BitVec 13)
    (h : decode (fetch32 m) = .bge a b imm) :
    step m = m.setPc (if (m.rget a).slt (m.rget b) then m.pc + 4 else m.pc + imm.signExtend 64) := by
  simp only [step, h]

theorem step_bgeu (m : State) (a b : Nat) (imm : BitVec 13)
    (h : decode (fetch32 m) = .bgeu a b imm) :
    step m = m.setPc (if (m.rget a).ult (m.rget b) then m.pc + 4 else m.pc + imm.signExtend 64) := by
  simp only [step, h]

/-! ## Immediate roundtrip for slot offsets. -/

/-- A small non-negative 12-bit immediate sign-extends to itself: the machine's
    `imm.signExtend 64` recovers `slotOff r` (kept `< 2¹¹` by `fnOk`'s
    `totalFrame ≤ 2000`). Derived through `toInt = toNat.bmod` — the direct
    `toNat_signExtend`/`signExtend_eq_setWidth` are `Classical.choice`-tainted in
    this stdlib, this route stays `[propext, Quot.sound]`. -/
```

### The one that bit me
`Reg := Nat` is an `abbrev`, but a **`Reg`-typed (in)equality is invisible to `omega`** — it silently drops the goal as non-arithmetic and reports a bogus counterexample from unrelated context hypotheses. `A i ≠ 2` worked only because `run_load`'s parameter is typed `Nat`; `A base ≠ A (base+1+j')` is `@Ne Reg` and omega ignored it. The fix (materialize `10+base : Nat` into the goal, or re-ascribe the negation as a Nat equality) is now documented so W4c/W5 don't re-hit it.

### What's left in W4 (and why I paused here)
